Well, back then it wasn't anxiety. I actually felt... almost brilliantly optimistic for a few weeks. I really didn't feel like I needed the sleep at all. It seemed like every obstacle in front of me was just trivial, easily handled. I had all this drive.

It's just not getting better on its own.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling completely ready to go during those early morning hours when you were waking up after just a few hours of sleep. I had all this drive. \n\nBut, I will say, if anyone questioned what I was doing or my plans... I snapped at them. I got incredibly frustrated. It honestly felt like they were trying to sabotage me, like I was the only sensible man in the room and they just couldn't see it. So, yes, I was very energetic and positive, but with a real sharp edge if anyone got in my way.\n\nDoctor: When you shifted from feeling brilliantly optimistic with all that drive to now lying awake with your stomach in a knot — was that a fairly sudden change, or did it happen gradually over time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. When you have all that energy, you aren't exactly paying attention to the calendar or tracking your moods. \n\nI just know that the drive sort of dried up. I couldn't tell you if it was an overnight crash or if it took a week or two to fade out. I just sort of realized one morning that I wasn't getting out of bed to work on my plans anymore... I was just lying there in the dark, feeling awful.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent period of feeling brilliantly optimistic with all that drive and energy, have you ever had other times in your life — maybe weeks or months apart — when you felt similarly energized, needed very little sleep, and felt like you could accomplish anything?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't remember ever feeling quite that level of energy or that complete lack of a need for sleep before. It was a first for me.\n\nDoctor: Right now, in this present moment, how would you describe your mood — are you still feeling that optimism and drive, or are you experiencing sadness, hopelessness, or a sense that things are pointless?\n\nPatient: Right now? It all just sort of shifted. One week I was running on all cylinders, and then... well, I don't know exactly how many days passed, but suddenly I was just down here in the dark. I really don't have a clear timeline on how that happened.\n\nDoctor: In the years before this recent period of high energy and optimism, did you ever have a time lasting several weeks or more where you felt persistently sad, down, or depressed — even if it wasn't as severe as what you're experiencing now?\n\nPatient: No, like I said before... I really haven't. Before all this recent stuff started, I never had a time where I was stuck feeling sad or down for weeks at a time. I've lived a pretty long time, and I've never had a stretch like that in all my years before this. My mood was always just... normal. This is all completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: Just to be absolutely clear: in all your years before this recent period of high energy started, have you ever experienced even a single episode of depression or low mood that lasted several weeks or more, even if it was mild?\n\nPatient: Look, I know you're just trying to be thorough, but... the answer is still no. Not even a mild one. \n\nIn all my 69 years before this whole thing started, I never went through anything like that. No stretches of being down for weeks, no mild depression... nothing. I've always been completely steady. Like I said, this is all completely new territory for me.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that high-energy, optimistic phase, did you notice any warning signs or shifts—like irritability increasing, or your thoughts starting to feel scattered—before the energy completely dried up and you crashed into this hopeless feeling?\n\nPatient: I didn't really notice anything like that. I don't remember getting irritable, or feeling like my thoughts were scattered right at the end. \n\nThe energy was just... there, and then it wasn't. I didn't see the crash coming at all. There weren't any warning signs that I can recall, the energy just eventually dried up.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the year or two before this recent period of high energy started, did you go through any stretch—even just a few weeks—where you felt noticeably more down, sad, or unmotivated than your usual baseline?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I feel like we keep going over the same ground here. No, I didn't. In the year or two before the high energy, or ten years before, or twenty... I never had a time where I felt sad or unmotivated for weeks. \n\nI've just never been a depressed person. Up until this current crash, my mood was always fine. I didn't have any dips or stretches of feeling down before the high energy started. It just didn't happen.\n\nDoctor: During that period when you felt brilliantly optimistic with all that energy and needed very little sleep, were you using any substances—whether prescription medications, over-the-counter drugs, caffeine, alcohol, or anything else—more than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't taking anything differently. I mean, I drink coffee in the morning, but I always have. I wasn't drinking more of it or anything like that. \n\nI don't really drink alcohol, maybe a glass of wine at a dinner party, but not during that time. And I wasn't taking any new medications, over-the-counter or otherwise. I wasn't using anything to give me that energy, it just... happened on its own.\n\nDoctor: In the few months right before you started feeling that brilliant optimism and high energy, did you notice any change in your mood at all — even something subtle, like feeling a bit more tired than usual, or less interested in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. Like I said, I've never really been a depressed person, so I didn't have any of those subtle changes either. I wasn't more tired, and I was still doing all my usual activities. Everything was completely normal right up until that high energy started.\n\nDoctor: After that period of high energy and optimism completely ended, how many days or weeks passed before you started feeling that hopelessness and emptiness you're describing now?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I... I don't have a specific number of days or weeks for you. It's hard to piece together exactly when the high energy completely stopped and the emptiness actually started. I just didn't keep track of any gap between them.\n\nDoctor: Going all the way back to your teenage years or even childhood — is there any time in your life, no matter how long ago, when you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or depressed for weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: No, never. I mean, I've lived a long life, so of course there have been bad days or times when I was upset about something specific, but... no. I've never had a time where I felt sad or hopeless for weeks on end. Like I mentioned before, I've just never been a depressed person. This current feeling of emptiness... it's completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: During those years before the high energy started, were there any times when you found it harder to concentrate at work, or when you felt less motivated to do things even though you weren't necessarily feeling sad?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my concentration was fine. I worked for a long time, right up until I retired, and I never really had issues getting my work done or staying focused. And as for motivation... no, I was always pretty active and engaged with things. Like I said, I just didn't have those kinds of problems before all this started. Everything was pretty steady.\n\nDoctor: I want to go back one more time to something important. I mean, my sleep has always been pretty regular. I'd get my seven or eight hours, wake up, and go about my day. I never had a time where I just didn't need to sleep for days on end, or where I felt unusually... wired or upbeat like that. This whole experience with the high energy, it's the first time anything like that has ever happened to me."
